# Northern Europe tables: batch 5a

For Kenny's review. Generated by `scripts/make-north5a.mjs` from `scripts/north5a/`: every entry the batch adds to the 15 modules of batch 5a (lands and territories, farmland and countryside, hills and valleys, rivers and inland waters, route names, sacred places, the six wild lands, seas and bays, coasts and islands, sea charts), by culture, module, region and era. Words sit in the pools `nMountain`… `nSacredD`, tagged with their culture; shapes are frames that read them. Stars and space are not touched.

**How to read it.** A word list reads `Word` with its eras after a semicolon (`AN-MD`: ancient to medieval; `EM-MO`: early modern to modern); the fantastic words (lists C) appear only in fantastic worlds. A frame uses `{N}` for the module's own word list and `{B}`, `{C}`, `{D}` for the shared ones; each module takes its frames on its three main kinds. Shapes with a region tag are listed under Regions.

## Celtic

Keys: celtic. Names with the generic first. A mix for fantasy and ancient settings: its own words, and each nation's at a quarter weight.

### Module nouns (§11.2): three lists per module, one per main kind

- **mountainNames, peak (nMountainPeak):** Hillfort Peak ‹AN›, Beacon Peak ‹AN›, Sky Father's Peak ‹h›, Great Peak

### Frames

- Own frames of the land modules (written for each module; shown for mountains): `{nMountainPeak} of {person}`, `{nMountainRidge} of the {tree}`, `{nMountainRange} of the {beast}`, `{nMountainPeak} above {town}`
- Own frames of the water modules (shown for coasts and islands): `{N} of {person}`, `{N} of the {seaCreature}`, `{N} of the {seaCreature}`, `{N} off {town}`
- Own route frames: road: `{N} to {destination}`, road: `{N} from {town}`, road: `{N} of {person}`, road: `Old {N}`
- Own sacred place frames: `{N} of {holy}`, `{N} of {place}`, `{N} of the {number}`, `{N} above {place}`
- Group frames (land / water / routes / sacred places): `{D} of the {tree}`, `{B} {D}`, `{C} {D}`, `{D} of the {beast}`, `{D} of the {seaCreature}`, `{B} {D}`, `{C} {D}`, `{D} of {holy}`; road: `{B} {N}`, road: `{D}`, pilgrim: `{N} to the {D}`, road: `{C} {N}`; `{D} of the Mother`, `{B} {D}`, `{C} {D}`, `the {sacredQuality} {D}`

## Regions

Region words: English words weighted per region (§4). Land tags are read by the land modules; water tags by seas and bays, coasts and islands and sea charts. A tag is drawn only for its cultures. The older words that read the Scandinavian regions (Norse-derived dialect words) are listed in the third table.

### Dictionary check (batch 5b, Part A)

Every word above was checked against standard English dictionaries (Collins, Oxford). Words not found there, or found only as a different sense, were replaced by their English sense:

| Replaced | By | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Ballybetagh | Tribal District | an Irish land unit, not in a standard dictionary |
| Cantred | Hundred | the same division, in a word the dictionary has |
| Callow | River Meadow | 'callow' as a riverside meadow is Irish-English only |
| Davoch | Ploughland | a Scottish land measure, not in a standard dictionary |
| Fank | Sheep Pen | Scots, not in a standard dictionary |
| Fermtoun | Farm Town | a Scots spelling |
| Toun | Town | a Scots spelling |
| Fianna | War Band | the legendary warrior bands, not an English noun |
| Heugh | Cliff | Scots, not in a standard dictionary |
| Knott | Knob | a Lake District spelling of knoll |

Kept: every other word in the table is a Collins or Oxford entry (regional entries such as brae, carse, linn, machair, combe, tor, wheal and zawn are marked as such there). The words added for region depth (dialect and landscape nouns such as bourne, weald, hirsel, corrie, links, bight) are standard dictionary words. A compound written as one word (oakwood, larchwood, fjordland, clanland) is two English words joined.
